Efficiency & Systems

Doing more with less — compression, quantization, and systems that make big models cheap.

← all themes

ICLR 2026

Reassessing Layer Pruning in LLMs: New Insights and Methods

Problem. Large language models require substantial computational resources for deployment, and best practices for layer pruning combined with effective fine-tuning methods remain unclear.
Approach. After extensive benchmarking, the paper shows that pruning final layers followed by fine-tuning lm_head and the remaining last three layers is remarkably effective, supported by gradient flow analysis.
Contribution. Surpasses existing state-of-the-art pruning methods by 5.62%-17.27% on Llama-3.1-8B-It, 2.36%-19.45% on Llama-3-8B and 4.34%-9.59% on Llama-3-70B.
layer pruningmodel compressionfine-tuninggradient flow analysis
ICLR 2026

MaskPro: Linear-Space Probabilistic Learning for Strict (N:M)-Sparsity on LLMs

Problem. Semi-structured (N:M)-sparsity offers hardware-friendly LLM inference acceleration, but existing approaches either use rule-based layerwise greedy search with errors or gradient-driven combinatorial learning with prohibitive training costs.
Approach. MaskPro proposes linear-space probabilistic framework learning prior categorical distributions for every M consecutive weights and introduces moving average tracker of loss residuals to mitigate high variance in policy gradients.
Contribution. Demonstrates superior performance with excellent scalability in memory efficiency and robustness to data samples for (N:M)-sparsity on LLMs.
model sparsitysemi-structured pruningprobabilistic learningpolicy gradients
ICLR 2026

Scaling Laws Meet Model Architecture: Toward Inference-Efficient LLMs

Problem. LLM inference cost grows prohibitively as models scale, and the trade-off between model accuracy and inference efficiency remains underexplored.
Approach. We introduce a conditional scaling law that augments the Chinchilla framework with architectural information, analyzing how hidden size, MLP-to-attention ratio, and grouped-query attention influence both inference cost and accuracy, then use a search framework to identify optimal architectures.
Contribution. Optimized architectures achieve up to 2.1% higher accuracy and 42% greater inference throughput compared to LLaMA-3.2 under the same training budget.
scaling lawsconditional scaling lawgrouped-query attentionarchitecture search
ICLR 2026

AutoSP: Unlocking Long-Context LLM Training Via Compiler-Based Sequence Parallelism

Problem. Existing LLM training libraries lack easy-to-use abstractions for long-context training optimization, forcing users to manually compose complex optimizations requiring in-depth expertise.
Approach. We introduce AutoSP, which automatically applies targeted optimizations: automated sequence parallelism and long-context-aware activation checkpointing.
Contribution. AutoSP increases training contexts by up to 2.7× on NVIDIA and 2.5× on AMD hardware at negligible cost to runtime performance.
sequence parallelismactivation checkpointingcompiler-based optimizationlong-context training
ICLR 2026

Why Low-Precision Transformer Training Fails: An Analysis on Flash Attention

Problem. Training transformers in low-precision settings with flash attention leads to catastrophic loss explosions, a long-standing and unresolved failure case.
Approach. We provide mechanistic explanation revealing that failure is caused by emergence of similar low-rank representations in attention and compounding biased rounding errors, introducing a minimal modification to flash attention.
Contribution. Our fix mitigates bias in rounding errors, stabilizing low-precision training and confirming our analysis of the underlying failure mechanism.
low-precision trainingflash attentionrounding error analysistraining stability
ICLR 2026

Efficient Resource-Constrained Training of Transformers via Subspace Optimization

Problem. On-device learning requires training models directly on edge devices to cut energy and protect privacy, but expanding neural networks create memory bottlenecks for backpropagation.
Approach. We apply subspace-based training to transformers through Weight-Activation Subspace Iteration (WASI), restricting training to fixed subspaces to mitigate memory bottlenecks.
Contribution. WASI maintains comparable accuracy while reducing memory usage by up to 62× and computational cost by up to 2×, achieving 1.4× faster training on Raspberry Pi 5.
subspace trainingmemory-efficient backpropagationon-device learningweight-activation subspace
ICLR 2026

LeSTD: LLM Compression via Learning-based Sparse Tensor Decomposition

Problem. Tensor decomposition methods for LLM compression face a dense core tensor bottleneck that grows polynomially with chosen ranks, capping achievable compression ratios.
Approach. We introduce LeSTD (Learning-based Sparse Tensor Decomposition), a two-stage framework that identifies a high-quality shared orthogonal basis then applies importance-based pruning to learn ultra-sparse core tensors.
Contribution. LeSTD breaks the compression ceiling imposed by dense cores, enabling significantly higher compression ratios than prior tensor decomposition methods.
sparse tensor decompositioncore tensor pruningimportance-based pruningllm compression
ICLR 2026

Vulcan: Crafting Compact Class-Specific Vision Transformers For Edge Intelligence

Problem. Compressed Vision Transformers for edge devices retain substantial class-irrelevant knowledge, resulting in suboptimal performance when only specific classes are needed for deployment.
Approach. Vulcan leverages knowledge disentanglement—where FFN modules encode class-specific and MHA captures class-agnostic patterns—by deliberately introducing redundancy through collapsing and low-rank constraints before pruning.
Contribution. Derives compact class-specific ViTs that significantly reduce size and computation while even surpassing original ViT performance on target classes.
vision transformer pruningknowledge disentanglementclass-specific compressionlow-rank factorization
ICLR 2026

DPad: Efficient Diffusion Language Models with Suffix Dropout

Problem. Diffusion-based language models suffer from high computational overhead by predicting all suffix tokens at each step while retaining only a fraction.
Approach. DPad restricts attention to a structured subset of suffix tokens via a sliding window and distance-decay dropout, maintaining fidelity while eliminating redundancy in a training-free manner.
Contribution. Delivers up to 61.4x speedup over vanilla diffusion LLMs while maintaining comparable accuracy on long-sequence inference.
sliding window attentiondistance-decay dropoutdiffusion language modelssuffix token pruning
ICLR 2026

StableToken: A Noise-Robust Semantic Speech Tokenizer for Resilient SpeechLLMs

Problem. Semantic speech tokenizers are fragile to acoustic perturbations, changing output tokens drastically even when speech remains perfectly intelligible.
Approach. StableToken uses multi-branch architecture with bit-wise voting consensus mechanism to merge parallel representations into stable token sequences.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art token stability under diverse noise conditions, significantly improving robustness of downstream SpeechLLMs.
consensus votingmulti-branch architectureacoustic robustnesssemantic tokenization
ICLR 2026

DSA: Efficient Inference For Video Generation Models via Distributed Sparse Attention

Problem. Attention in diffusion transformers for video generation scales quadratically with sequence length, bottlenecking inference.
Approach. DSA integrates sparse attention with distributed inference and carefully-designed parallelism strategies.
Contribution. On 8 GPUs, DSA achieves 1.43x speedup over existing distributed methods and 10.79x faster than single-GPU inference.
sparse attentiondistributed inferencevideo generation
ICLR 2026

KDP: Simplifying Representation Dynamics in Kernel Space

Problem. Large language models exhibit high similarity between consecutive layer representations, indicating computational redundancy that can be exploited for layer pruning.
Approach. KDP views the LLM forward pass as a discrete-time dynamical system operating on a slow manifold, projects representations into kernel space where transformations become approximately linear, then learns the inverse transformation enabling layer pruning.
Contribution. Both theoretical analysis and extensive experiments validate KDP's effectiveness and superiority over existing pruning baselines.
layer pruningkernel methodsdynamical systems
ICLR 2026

HiDivDrop: Vision Token Reduction in MLLMs via Late Injection and Differentiable Top-K

Problem. Multimodal LLMs remain computationally expensive due to quadratic complexity of processing vision tokens, and existing progressive pruning methods misunderstand layer roles and use rigid pruning schedules.
Approach. HiDivDrop introduces Late Injection to bypass shallow layers and feed tokens directly to fusion layers, plus a Concave Pyramid Pruning scheme with Early Exit that dynamically adjusts rates via inter-layer similarity and differentiable top-k operations.
Contribution. Compresses ~90% of vision tokens while matching original performance and accelerating training by 1.72x.
vision token pruninglate injectiondynamic pruning schedules
ICLR 2026

Is Finer Better? The Limits of Microscaling Formats in Large Language Models

Problem. Microscaling quantization shows anomalous degradation when block size decreases below a threshold, contradicting expectations that finer quantization should improve tensor representation.
Approach. The paper decouples quantization error sources experimentally and theoretically, proposing FP8 unsigned E5M3 format for microscaling weights that maintains performance while enabling efficient implementations.
Contribution. Identifies and explains anomalous microscaling behavior through interplay between narrow tensor distributions and limited dynamic range, proposing practical hardware-friendly solution.
quantizationmodel compressionnumerical formats
ICLR 2026

PLoP: Precise LoRA Placement for Efficient Finetuning of Large Models

Problem. Choosing effective LoRA adapter placement strategies across different model modules lacks principled guidance, with conflicting recommendations in prior work.
Approach. PLoP provides automatic identification of module types for LoRA placement through intuitive theoretical analysis, identifying where adapters should be placed for given pretrained models and tasks.
Contribution. Consistently outperforms or competes with commonly used LoRA placement strategies on supervised finetuning and reinforcement learning for reasoning tasks.
low-rank adaptationadapter placementparameter efficiency
ICLR 2026

Predicting LLM Output Length via Entropy-Guided Representations

Problem. Long-tailed sequence length distributions in LLM serving cause computational waste through excessive padding in batched inference.
Approach. ForeLen uses main model's hidden states with Entropy-Guided Token Pooling for static length prediction and Progressive Length Prediction for dynamic remaining length estimation during decoding.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art accuracy with 29.16% MAE reduction over baselines and significant end-to-end throughput gains when integrated with length-aware schedulers.
length predictionefficient inferencethroughput optimization
ICLR 2026

LD-MoLE: Learnable Dynamic Routing for Mixture of LoRA Experts

Problem. Existing mixture-of-experts approaches for parameter-efficient LLM adaptation rely on conventional TopK routing requiring careful hyperparameter tuning and assigning a fixed number of experts per token.
Approach. LD-MoLE replaces non-differentiable TopK routing with a differentiable routing function and closed-form solution, enabling adaptive, token-dependent, and layer-wise expert allocation with analytical sparsity control.
Contribution. LD-MoLE achieves superior performance on diverse benchmarks across Qwen3-1.7B and Llama-3.2-3B while learning token-dependent and layer-wise expert allocation patterns.
mixture of expertsparameter-efficient fine-tuninglearnable routingsparsity control
ICLR 2026

QuantSparse: Comprehensively Compressing Video Diffusion Transformer with Model Quantization and Attention Sparsification

Problem. Diffusion transformers for video generation require prohibitive computational and memory resources that hinder practical deployment, and naive combinations of quantization and sparsification cause performance degradation.
Approach. QuantSparse integrates model quantization with attention sparsification using Multi-Scale Salient Attention Distillation and Second-Order Sparse Attention Reparameterization to mitigate quantization-induced bias.
Contribution. QuantSparse on HunyuanVideo-13B achieves 20.88 PSNR with 3.68× storage reduction and 1.88× inference acceleration, substantially outperforming the quantization baseline Q-VDiT.
model quantizationattention sparsificationdistillationvideo generation
ICLR 2026

Boomerang Distillation Enables Zero-Shot Model Size Interpolation

Problem. Deploying large language models under diverse resource constraints requires training multiple independent model sizes, which is prohibitively expensive and provides only coarse-grained size options.
Approach. Boomerang distillation distills from a large teacher to a small student, then progressively reconstructs intermediate sizes by re-incorporating teacher layer blocks without additional training.
Contribution. Boomerang distillation generates zero-shot fine-grained model families whose performance scales smoothly between student and teacher, often matching or surpassing independently trained models of the same size.
model distillationlayer interpolationmodel compression
ICLR 2026

Reconstructing KV Caches with Cross-Layer Fusion for Enhanced Transformers

Problem. Transformer decoders' KV cache memory requirements become prohibitive at long sequence lengths, and existing cross-layer sharing methods underperform within-layer approaches like GQA.
Approach. FusedKV learns to fuse informative KV caches from bottom and middle layers into top-layer representations; FusedKV-Lite directly derives top-layer values from bottom-layer values and middle-layer keys.
Contribution. FusedKV reduces cache memory by 50% while achieving lower validation perplexity than standard Transformer decoders across LLMs ranging from 332M to 4B parameters.
KV cache compressioncross-layer fusionefficient transformers
ICLR 2026

BWCache: Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers through Block-Wise Caching

Problem. Diffusion Transformers for video generation suffer from inherent sequential denoising causing inevitable latency, limiting real-world applicability.
Approach. BWCache dynamically caches and reuses DiT block features across diffusion timesteps when feature variations fall below similarity threshold, using U-shaped pattern to identify redundancy.
Contribution. Achieves up to 2.24× speedup with comparable visual quality on several video diffusion models through training-free acceleration.
feature cachingdiffusion transformersinference acceleration
ICLR 2026

MT-DAO: Multi-Timescale Distributed Adaptive Optimizers with Local Updates

Problem. Distributed adaptive optimizers suffer performance gaps with infrequent communication; time-scale mismatch causes fast-moving momentum to decay too quickly.
Approach. MT-DAO uses multiple slow- and fast-moving first momenta tracking update dynamics across time scales with convergence guarantees.
Contribution. Eliminates DDP performance gap; for 720M model reaches target perplexity in 24% fewer steps and 35% less time; enables cross-datacenter training.
distributed optimizationadaptive learning ratelanguage model pretraining
ICLR 2026

DES-LOC: Desynced Low Communication Adaptive Optimizers for Foundation Models

Problem. Distributed foundation model training is bandwidth-limited because existing adaptive optimizers synchronize all optimizer states uniformly, tripling communication costs.
Approach. DES-LOC assigns independent synchronization periods to parameters and momenta, synchronizing parameters infrequently and momenta more frequently to balance convergence and communication.
Contribution. DES-LOC communicates 170x less than DDP and 2x less than Local Adam, achieving 1.3x–2.1x wall-clock speedups on 1-13B models while maintaining robustness to failures.
distributed optimizationasynchronous communicationadaptive learning rates
ICLR 2026

LouisKV: Efficient KV Cache Retrieval for Long Input-Output Sequences

Problem. KV cache significantly reduces memory overhead for auto-regressive models but introduces memory constraints limiting deployment in long-sequence scenarios.
Approach. LouisKV leverages temporal locality of critical KVs to trigger retrieval only at semantic boundaries and uses decoupled, fine-grained management that tailors strategies for input and output sequences.
Contribution. LouisKV efficiently manages KV cache for long-sequence scenarios with reduced computation and data transfer overhead, particularly benefiting reasoning models.
kv cache managementsemantic-aware retrievalmemory optimization
ICLR 2026

SMixer: Rethinking Efficient-Training and Event-Driven SNNs

Problem. Spiking Neural Networks face architecture design challenges and high GPU training overhead while sacrificing either performance or event-driven capability.
Approach. SMixer uses Spiking Mixer foundation architecture with Spatial-Temporal Pruning framework that eliminates redundant spike features across dimensions without trainable parameters.
Contribution. Maintains event-driven characteristics with competitive performance while significantly reducing GPU training overhead through adaptive token and firing-rate selection.
spiking neural networksspatial-temporal pruningevent-driven computation
ICLR 2026

PT$^2$-LLM: Post-Training Ternarization for Large Language Models

Problem. Post-training ternarization of LLMs faces challenges from training-free optimization, outlier-induced quantization difficulty, and dispersed weights.
Approach. PT²-LLM uses Asymmetric Ternary Quantizer with Iterative Ternary Fitting and Activation-aware Grid Alignment, plus Structural Similarity-based Reordering to handle outliers.
Contribution. Delivers competitive performance against 2-bit PTQ methods with lower memory cost while accelerating both prefill and decoding for end-to-end speedup.
ternary quantizationpost-training quantizationgrid alignment
ICLR 2026

Qronos: Correcting the Past by Shaping the Future... in Post-Training Quantization

Problem. Post-training quantization accumulates errors from previously quantized layers, limiting accuracy in extreme quantization settings of 4-bit or fewer.
Approach. Qronos iteratively alternates between error correction and diffusion via optimal update rules, with efficient formulation reducing peak memory by 18× and providing speedups up to 13.8×.
Contribution. Consistently outperforms adaptive rounding methods on Llama3 and Qwen3 families when quantizing weights, activations, and KV caches to 4 bits or fewer.
post-training quantizationerror correctiondiffusion update
ICLR 2026

LoFT: Low-Rank Adaptation That Behaves Like Full Fine-Tuning

Problem. Low-rank adaptation methods like LoRA underperform full fine-tuning in accuracy and convergence speed despite reducing trainable parameters.
Approach. Align low-rank updates with full updates by properly projecting optimizer's first and second moments into the same low-rank subspace as LoRA.
Contribution. LoFT substantially narrows performance gap with full fine-tuning, consistently outperforms LoRA without increasing inference cost and eliminates hyperparameter tuning.
parameter-efficient tuninglow-rank adaptationoptimizer alignmentfine-tuning
ICLR 2026

MoNE: Replacing Redundant Experts with Lightweight Novices for Structured Pruning of MoE

Problem. Mixture-of-Experts models require retaining all experts in memory during deployment, incurring substantial memory overhead that limits practical scalability.
Approach. MoNE replaces low-usage, low-variance experts with lightweight neural estimators called novices that approximate their outputs, factorizing expert redundancy by access frequency and output variance metrics.
Contribution. Outperforms baselines by up to 2.72 average zero-shot accuracy across nine tasks at 25% pruning ratio with only 0.14 performance drop on Qwen2-57B-A14B.
mixture of expertsstructured pruningexpert compressionlow-rank approximation
ICLR 2026

ARMOR: High-Performance Semi-Structured Pruning via Adaptive Matrix Factorization

Problem. 2:4 semi-structured pruning offers hardware acceleration but existing methods incur substantial performance degradation in LLM deployment.
Approach. ARMOR factorizes weight matrices into 2:4 sparse cores wrapped by low-overhead block diagonal matrices that act as error correctors; optimization via block coordinate descent with convergence guarantees.
Contribution. Consistently outperforms state-of-the-art 2:4 pruning on Llama and Qwen models; generalizes to N:M patterns and unstructured sparsity while retaining inference speedups.
semi-structured pruningmatrix factorizationblock diagonal adaptation2:4 sparsity
ICLR 2026

MoBE: Mixture-of-Basis-Experts for Compressing MoE-based LLMs

Problem. MoE-based LLMs require substantial memory for deployment despite computational efficiency; existing compression methods suffer significant accuracy drops even at modest compression rates.
Approach. Mixture-of-Basis-Experts decomposes expert weight matrices via W=AB where A is expert-specific and B is a linear combination of shared basis matrices across MoE layer; learned by minimizing reconstruction error.
Contribution. Reduces parameter counts of Qwen3-235BA22B-2507, DeepSeek-V3, and Kimi-K2-Instruct by 24-30% with only 1-2% accuracy drop (about 2% relative).
mixture of expertsrank decompositionbasis matrix factorizationparameter sharing
ICLR 2026

A Scalable Distributed Framework for Multimodal GigaVoxel Image Registration

Problem. Image registration algorithms have not scaled to match modern high-resolution image acquisition capabilities, limiting biomedical imaging analysis.
Approach. FFDP introduces IO-aware fused kernels and a distributed framework optimized for non-GEMM bottlenecks and tensor sharding to enable registration at unprecedented scales.
Contribution. The framework performs multimodal registration of a 100μm ex-vivo human brain MRI volume at native resolution in about one minute on 8 A6000 GPUs, achieving 6-7x acceleration while reducing peak memory by 20-59%.
distributed computingkernel optimizationtensor sharding
ICLR 2026

SPR$^2$Q: Static Priority-based Rectifier Routing Quantization for Image Super-Resolution

Problem. Low-bit quantization for image super-resolution struggles with component heterogeneity and information loss, especially under extreme compression.
Approach. SPR2Q injects compensation information through low-rank rectifier groups and introduces static priority-based routing that evaluates offline capability of each rectifier.
Contribution. SPR2Q achieves 0.55 dB and 1.31 dB PSNR improvements on Set5 under 4-bit and 2-bit settings respectively, outperforming state-of-the-art.
quantizationlow-rank approximationparameter routing
ICLR 2026

Lightweight Spatio-Temporal Modeling via Temporally Shifted Distillation for Real-Time Accident Anticipation

Problem. Real-time accident anticipation in intelligent transportation requires lightweight models suitable for edge deployment while maintaining predictive accuracy.
Approach. The framework introduces temporally shifted distillation enabling image-based teacher guidance, combines RepMixer spatial encoding with RWKV-inspired recurrent modules, and uses masking memory for robustness.
Contribution. The method achieves state-of-the-art on dashcam benchmarks while being 3-7x smaller than leading approaches and running real-time on NVIDIA Jetson Orin Nano.
knowledge distillationspatio-temporal modelsedge computing
ICLR 2026

SERE: Similarity-based Expert Re-routing for Efficient Batch Decoding in MoE Models

Problem. Mixture-of-Experts models require batch inference for hardware efficiency during serving, but batch decoding causes excessive expert activation that slows the memory-bound decoding stage.
Approach. SERE dynamically reduces active experts by re-routing tokens from secondary experts to similar primary experts based on input-aware similarity patterns, using a custom CUDA kernel integrated into vLLM.
Contribution. SERE achieves up to 2.0x speedup on complex reasoning benchmarks with minimal quality loss, providing a practical solution for cost-efficient MoE deployment.
expert re-routingdynamic expert skippingsimilarity-based expert reduction
ICLR 2026

Tequila: Deadzone-free Ternary Quantization for Large Language Models

Problem. Ternary weight quantization constraining weights to {-1, 0, 1} causes significant accuracy degradation because weights become trapped at the deadzone boundary with insufficient gradient signals.
Approach. Tequila reactivates deadzone-trapped weights by repurposing them as dynamic biases, providing continuous signals in the forward pass and meaningful gradients during backpropagation.
Contribution. Tequila achieves >4% accuracy gain over state-of-the-art ternary quantization methods on ARC benchmark, nearly matching full-precision performance with 3.0x inference speedup.
ternary quantizationdeadzone trapping mitigationdynamic bias repurposing
ICLR 2026

On-the-Fly Adaptation to Quantization: Configuration-Aware LoRA for Efficient Fine-Tuning of Quantized LLMs

Problem. Fine-tuning quantized LLMs on edge devices with heterogeneous capabilities requires configuration-wise fine-tuning for every quantization setting, which is computationally prohibitive.
Approach. CoA-LoRA uses a configuration-aware model mapping quantization configurations to low-rank adjustments without retraining, with Pareto-based search to optimize the training configuration set.
Contribution. CoA-LoRA achieves comparable or superior performance to state-of-the-art methods without additional time cost, enabling efficient adaptation to arbitrary quantization configurations.
configuration-aware low-rank adaptationpareto-based configuration searchquantization-aware fine-tuning
ICLR 2026

Fastcar: Cache Attentive Replay for Fast Auto-Regressive Video Generation on the Edge

Problem. Auto-regressive video generation has high latency on edge devices due to the substantial number of tokens needed for temporal frames and dominance of MLP operations in decoding.
Approach. FastCar exploits temporal redundancy in MLP outputs by using a Temporal Attention Score to determine when to replay cached MLP outputs from previous frames, and develops FPGA acceleration with Dynamic Resource Scheduling.
Contribution. FastCar achieves more than 2.1x decoding speedup with higher energy efficiency on edge devices, and improves sparse attention performance when combined.
cache replaytemporal attention scorefpga acceleration
ICLR 2026

SAFA-SNN: Sparsity-Aware On-Device Few-Shot Class-Incremental Learning with Fast-Adaptive Structure of Spiking Neural Network

Problem. On-device few-shot class-incremental learning on edge devices with limited resources requires memory efficiency while preserving knowledge of base classes and learning new classes.
Approach. SAFA-SNN applies spiking neural networks with sparsity-aware dynamic thresholds to preserve base-class knowledge naturally, uses zeroth-order optimization to handle spike non-differentiability, and enhances prototypes through subspace projection.
Contribution. SAFA-SNN achieves effective on-device few-shot class-incremental learning on standard and neuromorphic datasets while maintaining lower energy consumption than ANN-based approaches.
spiking neural networkszeroth-order optimizationsparsity-awareness
ICLR 2026

Learnable Sparsity for Vision Generative Models

Problem. Vision generative models achieve impressive results but suffer from high computational complexity and memory demands, and existing pruning methods require expensive retraining to maintain performance.
Approach. We propose a general pruning framework that learns a differentiable mask to sparsify models with an end-to-end pruning objective spanning all generation steps, plus a time step gradient checkpointing technique to reduce memory usage.
Contribution. Our method efficiently prunes 20% of parameters from SDXL and FLUX models in just 10 A100 GPU hours, outperforming previous pruning approaches with minimal retraining cost.
differentiable pruninggradient checkpointingparameter sparsity
ICLR 2026

Parallel Token Generation for Language Models

Problem. Autoregressive transformers require strictly sequential token prediction, and prior parallel prediction methods impose independence assumptions limiting representational power.
Approach. Inspired by inverse autoregressive normalizing flows, a framework jointly predicts multiple tokens by deterministically converting random variables into a token sequence within a single transformer call.
Contribution. Distilled models match teacher output for ~50 tokens on toy data and 5 tokens on coding within one forward pass, enabling parallel generation without sacrificing expressiveness.
inverse autoregressive flowsparallel token generationmodel distillation
ICLR 2026

Q&C: When Quantization Meets Cache in Efficient Generation

Problem. Combining quantization and caching for efficient generation is non-trivial due to calibration sample efficacy loss under cache operation and amplified exposure bias during sampling.
Approach. A temporal-aware parallel clustering (TAP) improves calibration sample selection per diffusion step while a variance compensation (VC) strategy corrects sampling distribution through adaptive correction factors.
Contribution. The hybrid method achieves up to 12.7x acceleration across diverse generation tasks while preserving generation quality.
temporal-aware clusteringvariance compensationpost-training quantization
ICLR 2026

CodeQuant: Unified Clustering and Quantization for Enhanced Outlier Smoothing in Low-Precision Mixture-of-Experts

Problem. Outliers in low-precision MoE models cause severe quantization errors and accuracy degradation; rotation-based smoothing leaves residual errors.
Approach. CodeQuant unifies quantization-and-clustering with learnable rotations for activation outliers and fine-tuned cluster centroids for weight outliers.
Contribution. CodeQuant achieves up to 4.15x speedup with significantly higher accuracy than state-of-the-art quantization across diverse MoE models.
quantization clusteringoutlier absorptionkernel design
ICLR 2026

Pruning as a Cooperative Game: Surrogate-Assisted Layer Contribution Estimation for Large Language Models

Problem. Layer-wise pruning of LLMs depends on static heuristics and fails to account for layer interdependencies, limiting effectiveness.
Approach. Formulates layer pruning as cooperative game where layers are players; uses lightweight surrogate network to estimate Shapley values and identify critical layers.
Contribution. Demonstrates consistent superiority in perplexity and zero-shot accuracy, achieving more efficient and effective layer-wise pruning.
pruninggame theoryshapley values
ICLR 2026

Can Transformers Really Do It All? On the Compatibility of Inductive Biases Across Tasks

Problem. Standard transformer design is assumed optimal but may be suboptimal for specific datasets; understanding task-specific inductive biases is unclear.
Approach. Optimizes transformer architecture for given datasets by learning most important non-linearities (GeLU, softmax) on held-out data, evaluates compatibility across tasks.
Contribution. Identifies task-specific architectures with dramatic improvements in learning speed and generalization; standard transformers rarely local optima but task-specific designs sacrifice universality.
architecture searchlearned activationsinductive bias analysis
ICLR 2026

Mixing Importance with Diversity: Joint Optimization for KV Cache Compression in Large Vision-Language Models

Problem. Large vision-language models expand KV cache creating memory bottleneck; existing compression methods focus on importance but overlook modality-specific semantic redundancy.
Approach. MixKV mixes importance with diversity for compression, adapting to head-wise semantic redundancy and selectively balancing diversity and importance when compressing.
Contribution. Consistently enhances existing methods across LVLMs with 5.1% average improvement under extreme compression and 8.0-9.0% gains on GUI grounding tasks.
kv cache compressionimportance samplingdiversity
ICLR 2026

PSDNorm: Temporal Normalization for Deep Learning in Sleep Staging

Problem. Distribution shift in biomedical applications across different subjects, institutions, and recording devices degrades model performance, and existing normalization layers ignore temporal dependencies in signal data.
Approach. PSDNorm leverages Monge mapping and temporal context to normalize feature maps while preserving auto-correlation in time-series signals, trained on 10K subjects across 10 datasets.
Contribution. PSDNorm achieves state-of-the-art performance on unseen datasets while being 4-times more data-efficient than BatchNorm on sleep staging tasks.
monge mappingtemporal normalizationdistribution shift handling
ICLR 2026

Octax: Accelerated CHIP-8 Arcade Environments for Reinforcement Learning in JAX

Problem. RL research needs scalable, diverse environments that are computationally tractable, but traditional CPU-bound video game emulators like Atari are expensive and poorly suited for large-scale GPU-based experimentation.
Approach. Octax implements classic arcade games (CHIP-8 based) entirely in JAX, creating GPU-native environments with orders-of-magnitude speedups and modular design for easy extension via LLMs.
Contribution. Octax enables massive-scale RL training on GPUs while demonstrating significant improvements in training speed and scalability compared to CPU emulators like traditional Atari.

ICLR 2026

Study of Training Dynamics for Memory-Constrained Fine-Tuning

Problem. Fine-tuning large neural networks within strict memory constraints is challenging, and static channel selection lacks awareness of data-specific importance patterns.
Approach. TraDy combines architecture-dependent layer selection with dynamic stochastic channel resampling between epochs, optimizing both memory use and gradient approximation quality.
Contribution. TraDy achieves up to 99% activation sparsity, 95% weight derivative sparsity, and 97% FLOPs reduction for weight derivatives while maintaining state-of-the-art performance on downstream tasks.
layer importancedynamic channel selectiongradient sparsity
ICLR 2026

AdaCache: Adaptive Caching and Context Augmentation for Efficient LLM Serving

Problem. RAG systems incur substantial overhead from processing frequently repeated text chunks and apply uniform retrieval depth regardless of query complexity, wasting computation.
Approach. AdaCache uses cache-aware partial recomputation profiling attention patterns for selective cache variants, and adaptive context augmentation dynamically determines optimal retrieval depth via lightweight confidence estimation.
Contribution. AdaCache delivers substantial improvements in Time-To-First-Token compared to state-of-the-art RAG caching systems while preserving generation quality.
adaptive cachingattention profilingretrieval optimization
ICLR 2026

TokenSeek: Memory Efficient Fine Tuning via Instance-Aware Token Ditching

Problem. Fine-tuning LLMs incurs high training memory costs dominated by activations, and existing data-agnostic optimization strategies are ineffective and unstable.
Approach. TokenSeek performs instance-aware token seeking and ditching to dynamically identify and remove less important tokens per example, reducing activation memory across transformer layers.
Contribution. TokenSeek requires only 14.8% of memory on Llama3.2 1B while achieving on-par or better fine-tuning performance; interpretable token seeking reveals insights for future token efficiency research.
token ditchinginstance-aware selectionactivation reduction
ICLR 2026

SpecBranch: Speculative Decoding via Hybrid Drafting and Rollback-Aware Branch Parallelism

Problem. Speculative decoding is constrained by serialized execution causing mutual waiting between draft and target models, limiting parallelism and efficiency.
Approach. SpecBranch adapts branch prediction mechanisms from CPUs: parallel speculative branches hedge against rejections, with adaptive draft lengths combining implicit draft confidence and explicit target feature reuse.
Contribution. SpecBranch achieves 1.8x-4.5x speedups over auto-regressive decoding and reduces rollback tokens by 50% on poorly-aligned models while maintaining identical sampling distribution.
branch parallelismspeculative decodingadaptive drafting
ICLR 2026

Demystifying and Enhancing the Efficiency of Large Language Model Based Search Agents

Problem. LLM-based search agents suffer efficiency bottlenecks: exact search incurs overhead, coarse retrieval requires extra reasoning, and system design inefficiencies cause cascading latency.
Approach. SearchAgent-X uses high-recall approximate retrieval with priority-aware scheduling and non-stall retrieval to eliminate waiting and scheduling inefficiencies.
Contribution. SearchAgent-X achieves up to 3.4x higher throughput and 5x lower latency compared to vLLM and HNSW-based systems without sacrificing generation quality.
approximate retrievalpriority schedulingretrieval optimization
ICLR 2026

MergOPT: A Merge-Aware Optimizer for Robust Model Merging

Problem. Model merging overlooks the role of the fine-tuning process, focusing only on parameter conflicts at merging stage, leading to significant post-merge performance degradation and instability.
Approach. MergOPT formulates model merging as distributionally robust optimization where parameters of other experts are viewed as adversarial merge-offsets, injecting merge-induced parameter shifts into fine-tuning weight updates to stabilize the loss landscape.
Contribution. Across four LLMs and one vision model, MergOPT consistently outperforms standard fine-tuning with average relative gain of 3.5% and maximum gain of 9.5% across merging strategies when merging seven experts.

Contribution. BLADE achieves 14.10x end-to-end inference acceleration on Wan2.1-1.3B and 8.89x speedup on CogVideoX-5B with consistent quality maintenance.

Contribution. KVTC achieves up to 20x compression while maintaining accuracy on reasoning and long-context tasks across Llama 3.1, Mistral-NeMo, and R1-Qwen 2.5.
kv cache compressionquantizationentropy codingtransform coding

Learning to Parallel: Accelerating Diffusion Large Language Models via Adaptive Parallel Decoding

LaplacianFormer:Rethinking Linear Attention with Laplacian Kernel

Problem. The quadratic complexity of softmax attention prevents Transformers from scaling to high-resolution vision tasks, and linear attention approximations using Gaussian kernels lack theoretical grounding and oversuppress mid-range interactions.
Approach. LaplacianFormer replaces softmax with a principled Laplacian kernel, introduces a provably injective feature map, and uses Nyström approximation with Newton-Schulz iteration for efficient computation.
Contribution. LaplacianFormer achieves strong performance-efficiency trade-offs on ImageNet while improving attention expressiveness compared to existing linear attention variants.

DASH: Deterministic Attention Scheduling for High-throughput Reproducible LLM Training

Problem. Deterministic attention implementations like FlashAttention-3 suffer 37.9% throughput reduction on backward passes because gradient accumulation must serialize to guarantee numerical consistency, causing hardware underutilization.
Approach. DASH formulates the backward pass as a scheduling problem on a directed acyclic graph and derives optimal schedules including Descending Q-Tile Iteration and Shift Scheduling to minimize critical path length.
Contribution. Improves attention backward pass throughput by up to 1.28× compared to baseline, significantly advancing reproducible LLM training efficiency while maintaining determinism.

Bridging Draft Policy Misalignment: Group Tree Optimization for Speculative Decoding

Problem. Speculative decoding accelerates LLM inference by verifying multiple draft tokens in parallel, but existing training objectives optimize only greedy draft paths while decoding uses a tree policy that re-ranks multiple branches.
Approach. Group Tree Optimization (GTO) aligns training with decoding-time tree policy through Draft Tree Reward measuring expected acceptance length and Group-based Draft Policy Training using PPO-style surrogate loss along the longest accepted sequence.
Contribution. GTO increases acceptance length by 7.4% and yields additional 7.7% speedup over prior state-of-the-art, with proven convergence that increasing Draft Tree Reward improves acceptance length and speedup.

SparseD: Sparse Attention for Diffusion Language Models

Robust Training of Neural Networks at Arbitrary Precision and Sparsity

Problem. Quantization and sparsification introduce discontinuous operations harmful to backpropagation, particularly in ultra-low precision and sparse regimes, due to the absence of proper gradient paths learning robustness to quantization noise.
Approach. Quantization is explicitly modeled as additive noise with a denoising dequantization transform derived from ridge regression, creating an explicit corrective gradient path; sparsification is treated as special quantization zeroing small values.
Contribution. The unified framework trains models at arbitrary precisions and sparsity levels with state-of-the-art results including stable A1W1 and sub-1-bit networks, mapping efficiency frontiers for modern LLMs.

ICaRus: Identical Cache Reuse for Efficient Multi-Model Inference

Problem. Multi-model inference systems experience explosive Key-Value cache memory consumption as each model maintains separate caches for identical prompts, forcing eviction and recomputation.
Approach. Identical Cache Reuse (ICaRus) allows multiple models to share identical KV caches by decomposing decoder-only Transformers into a logical encoder and decoder, fine-tuning only the decoder while freezing the encoder.
Contribution. ICaRus eliminates cache memory explosion and redundant recomputation while enabling cross-model reuse of KV caches for new tokens, achieving both efficiency and scalability.

Contribution. Achieves minimax convergence rates with O(n log n) complexity; processes tens of billions of samples within minutes on GPU.
kernel methodsfourier transformfast algorithms

Contribution. FedEMoE significantly improves accuracy on heterogeneous models across statistical and model heterogeneity settings.
mixture of expertsfederated learningweight spectrum analysis

Problem. Post-training quantization for W4A4 LLMs remains open, and existing methods designed for INT4 formats suffer from incompatibility with emerging MXFP4 format due to its power-of-two block scaling.
Approach. Analyzes the fundamental mismatch between MXFP4's power-of-two block scaling and global rotation-based optimization; proposes a simple block rotation strategy adapting rotation-based methods to MXFP4.
Contribution. Provides first in-depth analysis of rotation-based quantization incompatibility with MXFP4 and achieves substantial accuracy improvements across diverse LLMs through block rotation adaptation.
post-training quantizationMXFP4 formatblock rotationW4A4 quantization

Approach. UniSparse introduces composite tokens as compact multi-granularity contextual representations, enabling dynamic sparse attention through multi-granularity compression and block-level selection with hardware-friendly GPU execution.
Contribution. UniSparse achieves 99% of full-attention accuracy with up to 2.61× faster attention computation than FlashAttention across multiple modalities and tasks including synthetic and real-world applications.

Zeroth-Order Forward-Only SNN Training Inspiring Neuromorphic On-Chip Learning

Problem. Spiking neural networks (SNNs) have learning potential for on-chip edge computing but zeroth-order methods applied to SNNs are non-trivial due to step-function spiking activation.
Approach. The paper proposes Subspace-based Zeroth-Order (SZO) optimization leveraging the intrinsic low-dimensional structure of SNN optimization trajectories to substantially enhance zeroth-order learning efficacy.
Contribution. SZO achieves accuracy comparable to first-order methods with faster learning speed on model training, continual training, and unsupervised adaptation for neuromorphic on-chip learning.

On Efficient Scaling of GNNs via IO-Aware Layers Implementations

Problem. Graph Neural Networks are bottlenecked by sparse, irregular memory access patterns that limit scalability, and existing frameworks materialize edge-wise intermediates increasing memory traffic.
Approach. The paper takes an I/O-centric view, identifying three kernel families (SpMM-based convolutions, reduction-based aggregations, attention-based layers), and develops fused GPU kernels reducing data movement and improving locality.
Contribution. Fused attention kernels achieve up to 3.9× speedup for Graph Transformer with median 1.6×, and up to 8.5× for GATv2 with 76× peak memory reduction, validated across diverse graph sizes.

ProjQ: Project-and-Quantize for Adapter-Aware LLM Compression

Problem. Applying Post-Training Quantization followed by Low-Rank Adaptation sequentially is problematic because PTQ leaves behind random noise spread across weights that LoRA cannot easily fix.

Contribution. ProjQ achieves up to 2x lower evaluation loss for compensation compared to standard PTQ and matches the performance of standard 4-bit baselines on language modeling with only 3 bits.

RACER: Risk-Aware Calibrated Efficient Routing for Large Language Models

Problem. Efficiently routing queries to optimal LLMs requires single-model selection but routing systems are susceptible to misrouting that degrades performance.
Approach. RACER formulates LLM routing as the alpha-VOR problem to minimize expected model set size while controlling misrouting risk, constructing nested model sets via augmented scoring and finite-sample concentration bounds to calibrate abstention thresholds.
Contribution. RACER achieves rigorous distribution-free risk control on unseen test data in a post-hoc and model-agnostic manner, with theoretical guarantees and consistent downstream accuracy improvements across benchmarks.
